### Page 1

Macquarie Equity Research
26 October 2026
Nan Ya Plastics
Mix upgrade accelerating
Key Points
• NPC reported Sep revenue up 55% YoY while net profit surged over 9x
YoY to TWD35.1bn, with Sep DPS above 2Q26 DPS.
• We expect a rising high-end product contribution to accelerate Low Dk/
Low CTE glass fibre sales next year.
• Our industry checks suggest that CCL supply will remain tight due to
glass fibre shortages.
• Upside to our 4Q26 forecast. NPC reported strong Sep earnings
following a required disclosure on unusual share-price movements.
Revenue rose 55% YoY to TWD34.4bn, while net profit surged over
9x to TWD35.1bn. Sep EPS reached TWD4.42, above 2Q26 quarterly
EPS of TWD3.37. Mgmt attributed the strength mainly to electronic
materials, including high-end CCL benefiting from AI demand. We
estimate Sept EBIT at TWD8–9bn, ~45% of 3Q26 EBIT, implying upside to
our TWD20.3bn 4Q EBIT estimate.
• Rising high-end CCL contribution in 2027/28. The company refuted
market rumours that its M9 material had failed customer validation,
noting that advanced CCL expansion remains on track, with new capacity
fully dedicated to high-end products. We expect stronger growth in Low
Dk/Low CTE glass fibres, with high-end products reaching 40% of the mix
vs 10% this year as CSP orders ramp up next year. We also expect M9
CCL to pass validation in 1H27.
• Q-Cloth and PTFE not ready yet. Our checks at Taiwan TPCA Show
2026 with glass fibre and CCL players suggest demand is multiple times
industry capacity, with glass-fibre supply the key bottleneck given long
capacity and weaving-machine ramp-up times. As producers shift
capacity towards Low Dk/T-Cloth, E-Cloth supply should remain tight. Q-
Cloth remains constrained by high costs and poor flexibility, while Low
Dk glass fibre with HC Resin can deliver similar performance. PTFE is
also unlikely to threaten glass fibre near term given its low mechanical
strength.
Earnings changes: No changes.
Valuation: We maintain our NT$506.00 TP based on an unchanged 20x
FY27E PER.
Catalysts: 2H26 high-end CCL ramp-up and capacity expansion, glass fibre
capacity additions, and stronger equity-method earnings from 2027.
Investment Thesis and Recommendation
We expect high-end CCL qualifications, mix upgrades and utilisation gains
to drive earnings growth, supported by recovering legacy businesses and
higher associate earnings.

Macquarie Equity Research Nan Ya Plastics
Key Risks to Investment Thesis
• Slower-than-expected AI data centre capex.
• Delays in, or failure of, high-end CCL qualification.
• Weaker-than-expected ASP and utilisation ramp.
• Normalisation of Middle East EG supply
• Earnings volatility at Nan Ya Technology and Formosa
Petrochemical.
Company Description
Nan Ya Plastics is a core member of the Formosa Plastics
Group. The company operates four business lines, including

plastics, chemicals, polyester, and electronic materials,
across Taiwan, China, and the US. Nan Ya also holds equity-
method stakes in Nan Ya Technology (2408 TT) and Formosa
Petrochemical (6505 TT), and consolidates PCB exposure
through Nan Ya PCB (8046 TT).

Key Quant Findings
The quant model currently holds a reasonably positive
view on Nan Ya Plastics. The strongest style exposure is
Price Momentum, indicating this stock has had strong
medium to long term returns which often persist into
the future. The weakest style exposure is Profitability,
indicating this stock is not efficiently converting
investments to earnings; proxied by ratios like ROE or ROA.

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113) 金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

家登(3680)：EUV POD、中國 FOUP 雙軌成長，展望正向 
建議：維持買進

一、3Q26 營收創高，EUV POD 放量與產品組合優化推
升獲利 
家登 3Q26 營收 27.1 億元 ，+16.9% QoQ，+62.9% YOY，
受惠 EUV POD 及 FOUP 出貨同步成長，營運規模持續
擴大，除台灣主要客戶需求穩健外，美系大客戶出貨量
亦大幅增加，帶動高單價產品營收貢獻，另中國市場
FOUP 需求回溫 ， 多個客戶同步拉貨挹注營收 。 考量EUV 
POD 及中國FOUP 均為影響公司獲利結構的重要產品，
預期在出貨規模擴大、產品組合維持有利的情況下，家
登 3Q26 營收與獲利可望同步展現強勁成長。在產能利
用率提升、加上產品組合優化下，預估毛利率上升至
43.9%， 季增1.2%、 年增4.8%， 營業利益達5.3 億元，
營業利益率 19.7%，季增 2.4%、年增 10.6%，稅後歸
屬母公司淨利 4.2 億元，EPS 4.41 元。 
二、4Q26 營運維持高檔 
展望 4Q26，EUV POD 及 FOUP 需求延續，加上台灣
大客戶先進製程產能擴張，帶動載具需求續，預估家登
4Q26 營收 27.7 億元，+2.3% QoQ，+22.0% YoY。預
期稼動率及生產效率提升將改善毛利率，惟考量海外布
局、折舊攤提、人力擴編等因子下，以保守態度估計毛
利率略提升至 44.2%，季增 0.3 百分點，年增 0.9 百分
點 ， 不排除後續上修空間，預估營益率19.9%，季減 0.2
百分點 ， 年減增2.4 百分點 ， 歸屬母公司淨利4.2 億元，
EPS 為 4.35 元。 
三、先進製程擴產和中國 FOUP 回溫為公司成長雙動能 
家登的光罩載具以 EUV POD 為主要成長引擎，隨 AI
發展帶動先進製程光罩層數、客戶開案量及載具汰換需
求皆顯著增加，預期 EUV POD 用量穩健提升，就產業
端觀察 ， 台灣大客戶先進製程節點已佔其整體營收79%，
且投資金額、規模持續擴大，而 ASML 最新法說也顯示
EUV 設備需求強勁，為 EUV 供應鏈的中長期成長提供
支撐，另外，除台灣主要客戶需求穩健外，今年美系客
戶出貨量亦大幅增加，預期 2027 年延續有機成長。晶
圓載具方面，中國 FOUP 需求明顯回溫，且客戶組成較
過往更加多元，整體貢獻有望回到前波高峰。 
四、先進製程投資延續，2026-2027 年成長可期 
AI 發展推動先進製程進入一波新投資循環，ASML 近期
法說會表示，EUV 設備需求維持高檔，直至 2028 年都
供不應求，已有客戶針對 2029 年後訂單展開討論，而
台灣大客戶亦提及 2030 年將導入 High-NA EUV 進行量
產，我們認為 AI 相關需求不墜，可支持家登 EUV POD
中長期需求，惟實際營收貢獻仍視客戶開案、光罩使用
量及載具採購進度而定。獲利方面，內部效率提升與成
本反映有助改善部分產品利潤，預估 2026/2027 年營收
為 96.5/117.1 億元，+31.4%/21.3% YoY，EPS 
16.83/22.70 元，目前股價依 2026 年 EPS 推估，約位
於 30.5 倍本益比，維持買進評等，目標價615 元(35.0x 
3Q26-2Q27PER)。

隨客製化需求持續成長、柬埔寨廠人力恢復至5,000人並開始協助
客戶回補庫存，帶動3Q26營收創下歷史新高，且受惠於營收規模
擴大、產能利用率提升及關稅返還挹注，淨利率有望創高。展望後
市，公司持續擴增墨西哥產能，員工人數已達1,800人，後續預計
每年增加約 500 人，以改善交期並滿足客製化窗簾訂單成長需求；
越南新廠預計 2027年初投產，第一期規劃員工約 1,000人，可望
進一步擴大客製化產品產能與市佔率。市場需求方面，儘管美國市
場面臨高通膨及消費降級壓力，但公司憑藉穩健的財務結構，客製
化產品與同業價差已擴大至 30%～40%，具價格競爭優勢，有助
於提升市佔率及整體訂單量，預期維持 mid-single digit成長；固
定尺寸則透過新品推出，支撐營收維持 low-single digit成長；歐
洲市場方面，隨荷蘭、比利時及丹麥等地的覆蓋率提升，可望延續
high-single digit 成長。獲利方面，受惠於關稅稅率降至 20%、
漲價及營收規模效益逐步顯現，預期毛利率將延續成長趨勢。綜合
以上，研究部預估 2026、2027 年 EPS 為 23.92、27.91 元，以
16倍本益比評價，給予目標價 450元，投資建議為買進。  
 近期投資評等  2Q26 毛利率創高，惟油價及運價上漲壓抑獲利表現 
2Q26 營收 79.27 億元，YoY+2.86%，QoQ+8.52%，稅後損
益 13.91 億元，YoY+20.67%，QoQ-15.57%，EPS 4.75 元，
低於研究部預期。第二季為傳統消費旺季，營運表現較第一季
明顯回升。區域營收方面，北美市場受到消費降級影響，加上
柬埔寨廠人力短缺，營收YoY-2.1%；非美市場則受惠於荷蘭、
比利時及丹麥等地的覆蓋率提升，營收 YoY+19.8%，成為主
要成長動能。獲利方面，2Q26 毛利率 59.95%，YoY+0.7ppt，
QoQ+0.9ppt，受惠於客制化及歐洲市場營收比重持續提升，
帶動毛利率創歷史新高，惟油價及燃油附加費急漲，使費用率
升至 32.7%，抵銷部分毛利率改善效益，壓抑整體獲利表現。

公司客製化窗簾產能主要布局於中國及墨西哥，分別占整體產
能約 60%及 40%。中國廠員工人數約 4,000~4,500 人；墨西
哥則鎖定北美市場，截至 2026 年 9 月，舊廠與新廠合計員工
已達 1,800 人，預計逐年增加約 500 名員工，以改善交期問
題並因應市佔擴張需求，透過規模經濟及生產效率提升，進一
步優化整體獲利結構。柬埔寨廠則以固定尺寸窗簾為主，1H26
受缺工影響，出貨進度放緩， 6M26 起員工人數已由先前的
4,000 人回升至 5,000人，並開始協助客戶回補庫存至健康水
位。越南新廠預計於 2027 年初投產，聚焦客制化產品，第一
期規劃員工人數約 1,000 人，為中長期成長動能。

 同業價差擴大提升競爭力，歐洲市場成長動能延續 
儘管美國消費市場面臨高通膨、消費降級及關稅等壓力，但公
司憑藉穩健的財務結構，與同業的價差已擴大至30%～40%，
加上產品持續迭代更新，有助於提升市佔率及整體訂單量，預
估 2026~2027 年客製化業務可維持 mid-single digit成長；固
定尺寸則透過新品免打孔窗廉推出，提高平均售價，以維持
low-single digit 成長。歐洲市場方面，採線上直營電商模式，
專業人員到府量測與安裝服務，由於服務利潤率較高，已成為
主要獲利來源。目前已拓展至荷蘭、比利時、丹麥等市場，隨
覆蓋率快速提升，預期非美地區將延續 high-single digit 成
長。電動窗簾方面，價格為手動款的 2～2.5 倍，儘管目前佔
比仍為 low-single digit，但 CAGR 達 15%～20%以上，預期
未來營收占比有望提升至 10%，進一步帶動整體 ASP 及產品
組合優化。

料使用比重，降低對 PVC 價格波動的依賴，以減輕短期原料

3
成本變動對獲利的衝擊。此外，公司已於 2M26 啟動漲價計
劃，有助於緩解成本上升壓力，加上客製化產品營收比重逐步
擴大、關稅稅率降至 20%，預期全年毛利率仍將維持成長趨
勢。綜合以上，研究部預估  2026 年營收 322.33 億元，
YoY+7.56%，稅後損益 70.1 億元，YoY+6.19%，EPS 23.92
元；2027 年營收 351.08億元，YoY+8.92%，稅後損益 81.77
億元，YoY+16.65%，EPS 27.91 元。

 結論與建議 
隨客製化需求持續成長、柬埔寨廠人力恢復至5,000人並開始
協助客戶回補庫存，帶動3Q26營收創下歷史新高，且受惠於
營收規模擴大、產能利用率提升及關稅返還挹注，淨利率有望
創高。展望後市，公司持續擴增墨西哥產能，員工人數已達
1,800人，後續預計每年增加約500人，以改善交期並滿足客製
化窗簾訂單成長需求；越南新廠預計2027年初投產，第一期規
劃員工約1,000人，可望進一步擴大客製化產品產能與市佔
率。市場需求方面，儘管美國市場面臨高通膨及消費降級壓
力，但公司憑藉穩健的財務結構，客製化產品與同業價差已擴
大至30%～40%，具價格競爭優勢，有助於提升市佔率及整體
訂單量，預期維持mid-single digit成長；固定尺寸則透過新品
推出，支撐營收維持low-single digit成長；歐洲市場方面，隨
荷蘭、比利時及丹麥等地的覆蓋率提升，可望延續high-single 
digit成長。獲利方面，受惠於關稅稅率降至20%、漲價及營收
規模效益逐步顯現，預期毛利率將延續成長趨勢。綜合以上，
研究部預估2026、2027年EPS為23.92、27.91元，以16倍本
益比評價，給予目標價450元，投資建議為買進。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에_b94e5eb0.pdf

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450/KS) 
일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입 
2026-10-30

조선/방산. 한승한, RA. 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 1,300,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 760,000 원 
상승여력: 71.1%

Signal: 3Q26, 수출 물량 저조 및 국내 낮은 마진 물량 인식으로 어닝 쇼크 
Key: 4Q부터 내년까지 수출 물량 빠르게 회복하며 실적 성장 이어갈 전망 
Step: 연내 새로운 중장기 성장동력이 될 중동 수주계약 기대. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 수출 물량 납품 저조로 어닝 쇼크  
3Q26 연결 매출액 6 조 943 억원(-6.0% YoY, -34.4% QoQ), 영업이익 4,520억원
(-46.2% YoY, -66.9% QoQ)으로, 시장예상치(1조 1,615억원)를 대폭 하회하는 어닝 쇼
크 기록했다. ①폴란드 중심의 수출 물량의 매출 인식 시점이 4 분기로 이연되며, 수출 물량 
비중이 감소한 점이 컸다. ②내수 물량 또한 감소했으며, 특히 국내 물량 중에서도 상대적
으로 마진율 낮은 유무인 무기체계 초도 물량이 이번 분기에 집중됐다. ③내수&수출 전반
적인 지상방산 매출 비중 감소에 따른 고정비 부담이 컸으며, ④일회성 요인으로 대전 사업
장 사고에 따른 복구 비용 약 300 억원과 충당금 및 기타 비용 총 160 억원, 그리고 환율 
하락으로 영업이익의 약 10% 수준의 감소 효과 발생했다. 다만 4Q26 부터 이번 분기 이
연된 폴란드 중심의 수출 물량 인식이 반영되면서 큰 폭의 탑라인 및 이익 성장 시현할 전
망이며, 대전 사업장 또한 내년 상반기 가동 재개 예상됨에 따라 지상방산 부문은 전년 대비 
성장 폭 두드러지며 사측 가이던스 15% 매출 성장 충분히 달성 가능할 전망이다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 130 만원으로 하향 
한화에어로스페이스에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가 기존 160 만원에서 130
만원으로 하향한다. 최근 Peer 그룹의 주가 하락에 따라 멀티플 하향 조정했기 때문이다. 
폴란드 천무 유도탄 JV에 이어 현지화가 전제된 K9 3차 계약을 추진 중이며, 국내 장약 생
산능력 증설을 통한 유럽향 수요 대응과 미국 내 생산거점 구축까지 진행 중에 있다. 또한 
미국 차륜형 자주포 사업 시제 선정에 이은 궤도형 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다. 중동 국
가들의 방공무기체계에 대한 계약 논의는 매우 가시적인 상황이며, 사우디 MNG 사업 수주 
또한 시점의 문제일 뿐이다. 기존 유럽에 더해 중동은 현지화 전략을 기반으로 동사의 새로
운 중장기 성장동력이 될 지역이며, 올해 4 분기부터 해당 수주 모멘텀과 함께 큰 폭의 실적 
성장이 맞물리며 리레이팅 가능할 전망이다. 선제적 매수 기회라는 판단이다.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부_c581d324.pdf

### Page 1

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.

당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

## Document 288: EcoNews of 19 October 2026 (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 19 October 2026 (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
IMF ANNUAL MEETINGS
Resilience, but no relief. The IMF's latest global growth forecast is un-
changed from a year ago and from April 2026 (at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4%
in 2027), despite the energy shock, however with a different geogra -
phical breakdown: many energy importers and war-exposed countries
downgraded, and AI-exposed countries upgraded. Resilience was the

buzzword of the meetings, however it appeared mostly in the rear -
view mirror. Looking ahead, buffers are running thin, fiscal policy needs
urgent consolidation, and downside risks (from energy and AI exube -
rance) loom large. Global headline inflation is expected to rise to 4.7%
in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before easing back to 4.1% in
2027 (revised +0.2pp). Growth in advanced economies is revised up to
2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany), staying at 1.8% in 2027. Eu-

rozone growth is upgraded to 1.1% in 2026 (+0.2pp) and 1.2% in 2027.
US growth stays projected at 2.3% in 2026 and 2027 (+0.1pp). Germany
sees the largest G7 upgrade, to 1.3% in 2026 (+0.6pp) and 1.1% in 2027
(+0.1pp). France is the only major advanced economy downgraded, to
0.4% in 2026 (-0.2pp) and 0.7% in 2027 (-0.2pp). For more detailed
takeaways see Substack and this week’s EcoWeek editorial.
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Trade policy, competitiveness and budget agreements by year-end.

EU leaders asked the Commission to propose by year-end an instru -
ment allowing, in extreme cases, full closure of the single market to
a rival deemed to be attacking the EU economy. It is designed as a
“second-strike” deterrent against unfair practices and retaliation.
The Commission is also exploring extending the hybrid-vehicle safe -
guards approach agreed with China last week to other sectors such
as chemicals and machinery. On the "Made in Europe" label, vehicles

would need to be assembled in Europe with at least 70% of compo -
nent value (excluding batteries) sourced locally and with substantial
R&D in the EU. The UK would be included, provided London aligns with
EU trade defence measures. EU Council also called for decisive pro -
gress on the competitiveness agenda, with the single-market (inclu -
ding 26th regime) and simplification files to be wrapped up by year-end.
On the multi-annual financial framework, the objective remains a deal

by end-2026, so that legislation can be adopted in 2027.
EURO AREA
Moderate tightening does it. Multiple ECB Governing Council members,
speaking from the IMF Meetings in Bangkok, re-underwrote their
“moderate response” approach to the ongoing energy-driven infla -
tion surge. Accordingly, they signaled that additional rate action was
likely but not imminent, acknowledging that the rise in bond yields
was doing part of the job, and that there were no signs yet of second

round effects. Strengthening the case for moderation, the Eurozone in-
dustrial production fell in August for the second month running, by
0.5% m/m, pulled down by pharmaceutical output and motor vehicles.
That said, production in Netherlands surged to a new high (2.5% m/m
and 8.7% y/y), driven by AI machinery and equipment.
France
Business starts booming, insolvencies are stable, growth outlook is
downgraded. Business creations rose by 16% y/y in the 3 rd quarter

(+23% y/y in September), driven mainly by the digital sector and retail.
In parallel, business bankruptcies stabilised in the 3rd quarter according
to Altares, with a 0.5% increase y/y (compared with a 5.4% increase y/y
in Q2), and their impact on employment declined: 50,510 jobs at risk
in Q3, down 3% y/y (see our recent analysis). The Banque de France
halved its estimate for Q3 GDP to 0.1% q/q (same as Insee) due to
the manufacturing sector. Its monthly survey showed a sharp jump in

business uncertainty in September, driven by fuel prices and economic
deterioration (the most acute readings in industry and services since
April). The OAT-bund spread was rangebound (137-142).
Germany
Producer prices are rising. Wholesale prices rose 8.7% y/y (vs. 6.8% in
August) due to higher wholesale prices for energy products and inter -
mediate goods, particularly metals.
UNITED KINGDOM
Summer outperformance and kudos from IMF. Monthly GDP defied

expectations in August and posted a 0.1% m/m against a -0.1% consen-
sus. This marked a slowdown from July (0.4% m/m), but also a fourth
consecutive month of expansion. Industrial production underpinned
the advance: it accelerated to 0.8% m/m (consensus: -0.1%, prior: 0.3%)
thanks to pharmaceuticals. The IMF sees growth accelerating to 1.3%
in 2026 (+0.3 pp since July) and 1.2% in 2027. It also singled out the UK
among advanced economies for its efforts and progress in putting its

fiscal house in order.
UNITED STATES
Inflation heats up, manufacturing accelerates, but an October hike
is unlikely. CPI inflation exceeded expectations at 0.65% m/m in Sep -
tember (up 0.2pp), the fastest monthly pace since April. Accordingly,
the y/y rate climbed to 3.7% (up 0.3pp). The biggest upside surprise,
however, came from the core CPI: at 0.33% m/m (consensus: 0.2%, prior:
0.3%) and 2.6% y/y (consensus: 2.5%, prior: 2.4%); as services remained

sticky while goods accelerated to 0.3% m/m on autos and AI-related
items. Producer prices too rose well above expectations to 1.3% m/m
(consensus: 0.6%, prior: 0.5%) – the largest monthly increase since
March 2022 – and 6.3% y/y (consensus: 5.3%, prior: 5.6%). Industrial
production accelerated in September to 0.7% m/m (up 0.6pp) thanks
to utilities. Manufacturing returned to growth (0.3% m/m, up 0.5pp)
with gains broadening beyond the AI-related and aerospace sectors.

Retail sales excl. autos and gasoline, by contrast, slowed sharply to
0.2% m/m (down 0.9pp). During his speech at the IMF Meetings after
the hotter than expected inflation prints, Chair Warsh declined to hint
at an imminent rate hike. We continue to expect the next rate hike to
occur in December.
To watch this week:
- Developed markets Flash PMIs for October (Friday).
- Eurozone Germany September PPI, October ZEW (Tuesday), France Insee Business Climate (Thursday).

- UK August Employment data (Tuesday), September CPI and PPI (Wednesday), September Retail Sales (Friday).
- Japan September CPI, October PMI (Friday).

JAPAN
Producer price inflation eases. Producer prices fell for a second month
in a row in September, down a further 0.5% m/m, while the yearly rate
slowed for the first time since February (-0.8pp), though it remains
firm at 6.8%. The IMF forecasts growth at 0.7% in both 2026 and 2027,
broadly unchanged. The yen weakened past 158 per dollar again after

reports that the BoJ is leaning against a back-to-back October rate hike.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Emerging markets
Asia’s outlook upgraded, other regions downgraded.
The IMF upgraded its Asia growth forecast to 4.8% in 2026 and 4.4% in
2027. The largest upgrades are for economies well integrated into the
AI supply chain, notably Taiwan (+5.5pp to 10.7%), South Korea (+0.7pp
to 3.3%), Singapore, Vietnam, Malaysia and to some extent Thailand.
India is forecast to grow 7% in both 2026 and 2027 (fiscal year basis),

up 0.6 and 0.3pp from July on robust domestic demand.
Central Europe’s GDP growth is projected at 2.1% in 2026, slightly
lower than April’s forecast. In 2027, it remains unchanged at 2.3%.
Growth paths are diverse within the region.
In the Middle East & North Africa, GDP is expected to contract by 2%
in 2026. GCC real GDP should fall by 4.8% with only Oman expected
to post positive growth (4.1%). A marked rebound is expected for 2027
(MENA: 7%; GCC: 9.1%).

The growth outlook for Latin America remains subdued, revised down
to 2.3% in 2026 (from 2.4%) and 2.5% in 2027 (from 2.7%). However, in
its "International Trade Outlook 2026" published on 15 October, the
Economic Commission for Latin America and the Caribbean (ECLAC) ex-
pects the region to experience a trade boom in 2026: goods exports are
projected to surge by 20% in value terms (11% from price increases and
9% from volume growth), with trade to Asia (excluding China) soaring

by 32%, while intraregional trade growth lags behind at 8%. Yet, these
export gains are concentrated in commodities and low-value-added
manufacturing, limiting their spillover to broader growth. Stronger
trade is thus providing only a modest boost to the economy.
China
Explosive export growth, subdued inflation and credit growth. In Sep-
tember, export value growth surged to 36.3% y/y from 25% in August,
driven by semiconductors, computer equipment and other AI goods, as

well as green technologies. Export prices surged, especially for semi -
conductors, while total export volumes remained robust. Import value
growth also accelerated to 31% y/y from 28.2% in August, boosted by
both rising volume and rapidly increasing prices. The trade surplus
hit a new monthly record of USD 134 bn in September. CPI inflation
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, mainly because of a 14.9% rise in
vehicle fuel prices. Food prices continued to fall (-1.7% y/y) and core

inflation was stable at 1%. Producer price inflation accelerated to 5%
(the highest since 2022) due to rising commodity prices. Growth in to-
tal domestic credit outstanding (“social financing”) continued to slow
down, reaching a record low of 7.1% y/y in September. Bank loans to
households declined (-2% y/y) for the seventh consecutive month.
In Q3 2026, real GDP growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y (1.2% q/q) from
4.3% y/y (0.9% q/q), primarily led by industrial production (which rose by

5.1% y/y on average in Q3 vs. 4.6% in Q2). On the demand side, exports
were the key driver while fixed-asset investment continued to contract.
Retail sales posted a small pickup in September but remained flat
in real terms in Q3 as a whole. With growth reaching 4.6% y/y year-to-
date, China needs only a 4.2% growth in Q4 to meet the official target of
4.5%-5% for this year – which seems easily feasible. The IMF trimmed
its growth forecast by 0.1pp to 4.5% in 2026 and 4% in 2027.

Indonesia
MSCI launched a consultation on reclassifying the country from Emer-
ging Market to Standalone Market status, with a final decision due by
end-June 2027, citing opaque shareholding structures and suspected
coordinating trading. Since January 2026, MSCI had frozen Index ad -
justments and threatened only a downgrade to Frontier market status
(like Pakistan), but market participants argued that a Frontier reclas -
sification would not properly reflect the extent of the market’s pro -

blems. Over the medium term, the risk is heavy foreign capital outflows
at a time when net FDI no longer covers the current account deficit.
This leaves Indonesia reliant on foreign portfolio inflows and the ru -
piah exposed to a reversal in sentiment. On the day of this announce -
ment, the Jakarta Composite Index fell by 11.6%, its steepest drop since
January 1998. Among Asian emerging markets, Indonesia has posted
the worst stock market performance since the beginning of the year

(-40.7%).
COMMODITIES
Iran/Oil. More of the same. Brent oil prices have remained broadly
stable this week (0.5% at 107$/b on Monday). Renewed Iranian at -
tacks on tankers in the Strait and intensification of Houthis’ attack over
the Saudi territory feed the geopolitical risk premium on barrel prices.
But IEA announcement of an accelerated 100 mb oil release (above
the March initial target of 400 mb) and reports that the US may lift
sanctions on the Russian and Iranian shadow fleets have contributed to

crude price stability. The spread with physical barrel (Dated Brent) has
declined (6.7$/b on Oct 16th). Trump administration’s various attempts
to lower diesel prices have had a limited impact for now. European gas
prices (TTF) recorded another weekly rise (4.7% w/w to 88.4 €/MWh,
the highest level since Dec 22nd, 2022) on the back of the rising number
of attacks on vessels in the Gulf and persisting disruption in the Strait.
However, EU gas inventories reached 74% of full capacities on October

16th (i.e., almost at the 80% month-end target), which should reduce
support for TTF prices going forward.
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels

## Document 310: Geopolitical turbulence complicates airlines’ flight paths for 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/Geopolitical turbulence complicates airlines’ flight paths for 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 18

### Page 1

Global air travel growth is set to rebound to 5% in 2027, but
airlines face tougher conditions. Demand remains resilient, but
the fuel price shock will spill over into 2027, while new aircraft
supply remains constrained. This creates divergent fortunes
across airlines, segments and regions
Rico Luman
The aviation industry now faces a growing challenge to deliver proﬁtable
growth as capacity remains constrained, costs rise, and geopolitical risks
intensify

The fuel-price shock will continue into 2027. Jet fuel
could account for close to 35% of airlines’ operating
costs in 2026, up from 27% in 2025. Tight reﬁnery
capacity and low inventories are likely to keep prices
elevated even if oil prices ease.
European air fares will have further to rise. Hedging
initially protected many European airlines, but that cover
will progressively expire during 2027. US fares have

Budget airlines are particularly exposed. Fuel represents
a larger share of their costs, while price-sensitive

Premium carriers have more scope to raise fares without

Aircraft shortages will continue to constrain capacity.
Airbus and Boeing had a combined backlog of around

one-third to around 45%, with India standing out: its

Cost shock from Middle East disruption
most impactful
Air traﬃc to and from the Middle East has taken a signiﬁcant hit as

a result of the war, which has led to airspace closures and
passengers shifting to other destinations due to safety concerns.
And this comes on top of the Russian airspace, which complicates
operations. Traﬃc in this pivotal region on the East-West corridor
halved in March and April and was still 15% lower year-on-year in
August 2026. Before the war, the Middle East accounted for 9.5% of
global revenue passenger kilometres (RPKs). On balance, the

setback in the region shaved some 2-3 percentage points oﬀ global
growth with additional impacts elsewhere.
Despite a sharp increase in jet fuel prices, airline response remained
constrained, with most measures limited to cancelling certain
unproﬁtable routes rather than undertaking broader capacity

Jet fuel continues to trade high in Q4 2026
European jet fuel prices in $ per tonne (ARA)
Source: Reﬁnitiv, ING Research
Cost shock hit proﬁtability, but for some
airlines more than others

Jet fuel is often the largest or second-largest cost item for airlines.
In 2025, it accounted for 27% of total operational costs on average.
This share could move towards 35% in 2026 as a result of
persistently high jet fuel prices and elevated crack spreads, caused
by the loss of Middle Eastern supplies and tight global availability.
Further geopolitical developments remain highly uncertain. Even if
the Middle East conﬂict is resolved and oil prices retreat, tight

reﬁnery capacity and depleted inventories are likely to keep middle-
distillate prices, including jet fuel, elevated well into 2027.
High fuel prices impact proﬁts in US and
Europe, low cost carriers most vulnerable

### Page 7

Operational proﬁt (EBIT) in % per peiod
Source: Company reports, ING Research
Fuel hedging provides less cushioning in
2027; fares must respond
Airlines employ very diﬀerent strategies to protect themselves
against fuel price shocks, using derivatives based on crude oil or jet
fuel. European airlines generally hedged 40-90% of their jet fuel
consumption, cushioning the initial impact of the price shock.
However, the Middle East conﬂict has lasted too long to avoid a

signiﬁcant impact. Existing hedges gradually roll oﬀ, increasing
exposure to prevailing market prices. Ryanair, which remains (80%)
hedged until the second quarter of 2027, has already warned of
signiﬁcantly higher fares.
Outside, the picture is mixed, but hedge ratios are generally lower,
and airlines in the US and China, for instance, don’t hedge at all.
This means that two airlines operating similar routes can face very
diﬀerent fuel bills.
Airline tickets in the US responded much

faster to the cost shock than in Europe

Index ticket prices vs consumer price index (CPI, seasonally adjusted) in

Fares already sharply higher in the US, less
so in Europe
The fuel price shock will increasingly feed through into higher fares.
US airlines’ practice of retaining relatively high exposure to fuel price
volatility resulted in an almost immediate pass-through, with ticket
prices rising solidly in the months after jet fuel prices started to rise.

It is also easier to raise prices when domestic competitors face a
similar cost shock.
The situation is diﬀerent in Europe, where hedging provided more
initial protection. This also means fares may still need to rise
signiﬁcantly if fuel prices fail to fall. Coverage gradually expires as
2027 progresses, leaving airlines increasingly exposed to higher
spot prices.
Airline proﬁts hit, but industry remains
proﬁtable

### Page 9

Source: IATA, *ING Research based on IATA
More factors are driving up costs,
particularly in Europe
Jet fuel is the clear outlier this year, but wage and maintenance
costs are also rising faster than the average price level. Borrowing
costs and aircraft leasing rates remain another factor, although the
impact depends on individual airlines’ strategies and ﬁnancial
structures.
European airlines also face rising costs from the European Union
Emissions Trading System (ETS) for ﬂights within the European

Economic Area. In addition, fuel suppliers are required to blend
sustainable aviation fuel (SAF) under ReFuelEU Aviation, starting at
2% and rising to 6% by 2030*. SAF still trades at a signiﬁcant
premium. This puts a price on emissions, and in combination, this
adds some $300 per tonne of jet fuel in 2026 (BNEF, ING Research).
Meanwhile, France and the Netherlands have either raised or plan
to raise ticket taxes, particularly on long-haul ﬂying, while Germany

has scaled its levy back and Sweden has abolished it.
High fuel prices put more pressure on low-
cost carriers

then be in scope. **In 2025, the blend rate stood at 2.8%

### Page 10

Low-cost carriers are particularly exposed because fuel accounts
for a larger share of their cost base, and proﬁt bases are more
vulnerable. High hedge ratios initially cushion volatility, but these
hedges gradually roll oﬀ. The more challenging environment is
already visible in the performance of several European low-cost
carriers and will remain an important theme in 2027 if fuel prices
stay elevated.
Premium carriers have more room to manoeuvre. Demand for

premium seats (business, premium economy) is less price-
sensitive, providing airlines with greater scope to pass on higher
costs. Both United Airlines and Delta Airlines reported little impact
after price increases of 15-20% in the second to third quarters.
Passengers are booking later, but airlines continue to indicate a
strong propensity to ﬂy, especially in premium and premium-
economy classes. A minority also accounts for a large share of
ﬂights. In the Netherlands, for example, 25% of people account for

75% of journeys. These frequent travellers tend to be less price-
sensitive.
There has therefore been some demand destruction wiping out part
of the underlying growth. So far, though, the propensity to travel
has remained resilient.
Aviation remains proﬁtable, but margins hit
a post-pandemic low
The fuel shock has signiﬁcantly aﬀected margins. On average,
proﬁts in the global industry are expected to drop meaningfully
below 5%. Airlines including Ryanair, Southwest Airlines and AirAsia

have scaled back capacity on unproﬁtable routes as elevated fuel
prices persisted longer than expected.
According to global aviation consultancy Cirium, around 1% of
global air travel capacity will be removed heading into the Northern
Hemisphere winter of 2026-27. In less mature markets, maintaining
volumes while passing on the higher fuel bill is more diﬃcult.
Airlines may also prioritise less fuel-eﬃcient, older-generation
aircraft over capacity growth.

### Page 11

Industry headwinds could become a catalyst for further
consolidation, ranging from minority stakes to full takeovers.
Fares could retreat if fuel prices ease, but
don't expect too much
Dynamic and algorithm-based pricing is becoming increasingly
common. This helps airlines optimise already historically high load
factors (84% in 2026 so far) but can also contribute to greater fare
volatility, especially as passengers book later amid uncertainty.
If fuel prices ease in 2027, fares could drop back. Yet relatively

strong demand, constrained aircraft availability and general
inﬂation mean a return to early-2026 fare levels looks unlikely.
Cargo beneﬁts from high-tech demand and
supply-chain volatility
Air cargo continues to perform well as a fast transport option in an
increasingly volatile and less predictable world. Volume growth
remained almost 5% year-to-date, while freight rates remained
attractive for carriers.
While e-commerce drove much of the growth in recent years,

digitalisation and large-scale AI investment increasingly support
shipments of high-value technology products. Strong cargo
performance helped cushion pressure on airline results in 2026.
European carriers also beneﬁted from reduced available freight
capacity from Middle Eastern competitors.
Freighter capacity received a boost during the pandemic through
passenger-to-freighter conversions, but that boost has largely run
its course. New-generation A350 and Boeing 777 freighters should

add capacity in the coming years.
Aircargo volume resilient in disruptive
geopolitical environment

Future demand 2027 and onwards
Aviation’s centre of gravity is increasingly
shifting eastwards
The centre of gravity of global aviation is increasingly shifting
eastwards. Trips per capita are expected to see strong increases
over the next two decades, particularly in Asia. This matters even
more because these markets are far more populous.
Until recently, around 90% of the world’s population did not ﬂy in a
given year, but this is gradually changing. As a result, ﬂeet growth is

expected to be strongest in Asia, with its share of the global aircraft
ﬂeet increasing from around 33% to approximately 45%.

in India

### Page 13

Source: S&P, Airbus, ING Research
India is becoming a powerhouse for future
airline growth
Air travel is strongly correlated with GDP and household income. As
Asia’s middle class expands, the potential customer base for air
travel swells. For future aviation demand, this income eﬀect is even
more important than population growth alone.
Younger generations also appear keen to travel, adding to the
potential in countries such as India and Indonesia. Increasingly

internationally dispersed families provide another structural source
of demand.
India stands out here. Strong economic growth, rising household
incomes and a huge population oﬀer strong potential. Airlines are
positioning themselves accordingly, illustrated by historically large
aircraft orders from IndiGo and Air India. India’s aircraft ﬂeet is
expected to grow by around 8% annually on average over the next
two decades.
China and India are the growth engines of

global aviation in the decades ahead

### Page 14

Source: S&P Global, Airbus, ING Research
Supply-side developments
Aircraft scarcity keeps capacity tight and
lease rates elevated
Supply constraints have been curbing aviation growth since the
post-pandemic recovery and remain a major factor. The share of
stored aircraft dropped below 10% by the end of 2025, its lowest
level since 2019. Even several A380s (the largest aircraft) have
returned to service.
More than half of the global commercial aircraft ﬂeet is leased.

Tight capacity is consequently reﬂected in relatively high used-
aircraft values and lease rates.
Airbus and Boeing had a combined record backlog of around
15,000 aircraft by mid-2026. Production and deliveries continue to
lag order intake after seven consecutive years of subdued deliveries
from 2019 onwards. Demand for new-generation aircraft remains
strong, as illustrated by major recent orders from Avolon and
Turkish Airlines.
Production could ﬁnally return to pre-pandemic levels in 2027.

Chinese manufacturer COMAC, meanwhile, is making progress in
China in Asia, but Western certiﬁcation remains distant.

Why aircraft supply remains constrained
Production has still not caught up with pre-pandemic levels
and is unlikely to do so in the next ﬁve years. Aircraft
manufacturing is recovering, but delays and large backlogs
push delivery dates into the 2030s. Airlines are therefore forced

to keep older aircraft ﬂying for longer.
Suppliers are still struggling to ramp up. Six years after the
sharp pandemic downturn, aviation supply chains have still
not fully normalised. Stringent safety and certiﬁcation
requirements mean manufacturers rely on a limited number of
specialised suppliers. Engines and sophisticated cabin
components still have long lead times, while skilled workers
remain scarce.
Engine issues continue to aﬀect available capacity. The peak

impact of the Pratt & Whitney inspection programme after an
identiﬁed production issue has passed, but checks and
maintenance continue to constrain parts of the A320neo-
family ﬂeet and absorb scarce maintenance capacity.

Delivery catch-up is crucial for both
capacity and efﬁciency
New aircraft are critical not only to accommodate growth, but also
to improve eﬃciency. The average age of the global ﬂeet has
increased from around 13 to 15 years since 2019 due to production
constraints.
This matters increasingly as older units require more maintenance

at a time when skilled personnel and maintenance capacity are
scarce; they also consume signiﬁcantly more fuel and therefore
emit more.
Boeing is ﬁnally regaining altitude
supporting deliveries
Boeing is ﬁnally making a sustained comeback following an
unprecedented period of disruption stemming from the 737 MAX
accidents, the pandemic and subsequent production and quality
problems. It received certiﬁcation for its 737 MAX 7 and also the
737 MAX 10 recently. Boeing plans to increase production for the

family from 38 to 47 a month.

### Page 17

Higher 737 production should support deliveries in 2027 and
gradually bring pre-pandemic production levels back into sight. This
is hugely important for Boeing-dependent airlines such as Ryanair
and Southwest. Progress on certiﬁcation of the 777-9 is also
important for long-haul ﬂeet renewal.
Fleet renewal is currently the most
important decarbonisation lever
Air travel continues to grow, and associated emissions tend to
continue to rise further as well. Sustainable aviation fuels are

labelled as an important industry pathway to decarbonise, but
globally, blend rates still hover below 1% in 2026.
The closure of airspaces, too, has created ineﬃciencies and extra
emissions over the last few years, undermining gains elsewhere.
Around 63% of the global ﬂeet still consists of previous-generation
aircraft. Replacing them can reduce fuel consumption by 10-25% or
more, making ﬂeet renewal one of the most eﬀective near-term
decarbonisation levers available to airlines.

The challenge is to deliver proﬁtable growth
in a more disruptive world
For airlines, the challenge heading into 2027 is therefore not simply

ﬁnding suﬃcient demand. Relatively poor punctuality, with only
around 61% of ﬂights departing on time in 2026 year-to-date, is a
signal.
The globally operating industry faces more turbulence and
uncertainty amid intensiﬁed geopolitical risks. The challenge is to
deliver proﬁtable growth in a world of persistently constrained
capacity, higher costs and more frequent disruption.

## Document 312: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Bond Yields Ease

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Bond Yields Ease.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Bond Yields Ease
Date:    3 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Bond Yields Ease

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Bond
Yields Ease
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 25 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Jeffrey Hamilton / Unsplash

Bond yields eased in most major markets last week, but France was the
glaring exception. In stocks, the global AI selloff kept hitting the equities
markets that had gained the most this year, putting the US back ahead of
the All Country World ex-US ytd. That supports our downgrade last week
of Go Global to market weight, though the fundamental case for overseas
stocks remains strong. Here's more:
I. Global Bonds

### Page 2

Yields fell in five of the eight major bond markets we track (chart). The US
10-year Treasury yield fell to 5.00%. France's yield jumped 24bps to
5.02%, slightly surpassing the US’s. Yields also fell in the UK, Australia,
Germany, and Japan.
The spread between French and German 10-year yields widened 31bps
last week to 167.9bps (chart). Last week, we wrote that contagion from
France had eased for now. It’s back. Italy’s spread widened to 123.9bps

from 106.6bps. Greece’s widened to 105.0bps from 90.8bps. Italy’s 10-
year bond now yields 44bps less than France’s.
II. Global Stocks
Brazil remains at the top of the country ETF rankings mtd, up 14.3% in
dollars (chart). South Korea remains at the bottom, down 17.2%. The US

### Page 3

is down just 0.3%, while the All Country World ex-US ETF is down 2.8%.
Go Global no longer leads Stay Home this year. The US ETF is up 11.4%
ytd, ahead of the All Country World ex-US ETF's 9.5% (chart). Taiwan
(65.1%) and South Korea (55.6%) still top the ytd rankings, followed by
Brazil (34.1%). China (-15.6%) and Indonesia (-43.3%) are at the bottom.

### Page 4

III. Global Earnings & Valuations
The US MSCI's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap has
edged up to 65.0% (chart). The Emerging Markets (EM) MSCI’s share
slipped to 11.7% from 12.1% last week.
Yet the EM MSCI now accounts for 20.2% of the All Country World MSCI’s
forward earnings (chart). That’s more than Europe’s 15.0%. It’s the first

### Page 5

time that EM has overtaken Europe since the data began in 1995. The US
accounts for 54.4% of forward earnings, well below its market-cap share.
That mismatch shows up in valuations. The US MSCI’s forward P/E is
19.2 versus 12.2 for the All Country World ex-US (chart). The ex-US
multiple has dropped from 15.7 in January even as its forward earnings
soared. The EM MSCI trades at just 9.0.
IV. In Focus
(1) Brazil. Brazil’s MSCI stock price index is up 22.2% ytd in local

currency and 31.0% in dollars (chart). The real has gained 8.0% against
the dollar this year. In dollar terms, the index remains less than half its
2008 peak. Stocks rallied after Senator Flávio Bolsonaro took a surprise
lead in the October 4 first round. He held a narrow edge in the final polls

### Page 6

before the October 25 runoff against President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva. A
Bloomberg poll released on October 19 gave Bolsonaro 51.1% of valid
votes against 48.9% for Lula.
Valuations aren’t stretched. Brazil’s forward P/E is 9.2 (chart). That’s
below its 2025 high of 10.7. The catch is that analysts expect earnings to
grow 44.6% this year but just 0.6% in 2027.
Brazil’s central bank has room to ease. It has lowered the Selic rate to
13.75% from a peak of 15.00% (chart). Inflation was 4.6% in September.

That leaves a real policy rate of about 9%.

### Page 7

(2) China. China’s property bust is deepening. The Shenzhen Real Estate
Stock Price Index fell to 953.55, breaking below the floor that had held
since 2012 (chart). Last week, we wrote that we would worry if it did that.
Now we are worried. Real estate investment is down 20.6% through the
first nine months of the year. Exports continue to carry the economy.
Beijing’s stabilization efforts haven’t stopped the selling. The China ETF
(FXI) fell 4.5% last week. Chinese technology stocks have missed the AI

rally that lifted US tech (chart). The China Technology ETF (CQQQ) is at
40.26, far below its 2021 peak.

### Page 8

China’s bond market confirms the deflationary picture. The 10-year
government bond yield is down to 1.62% (chart). It’s one of only two major
yields that have fallen this year. China’s yield is now well below Japan’s
2.80%.
💡

log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end
of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

### Page 9

## Document 375: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-10-31)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-10-31).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-31
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

### Page 10

## Document 377: September CPI_ preview and analysis (2026-10-31)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ preview and analysis (2026-10-31).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-31
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
SEPTEMBER CPI PREVIEW
Setting the pace for the run into year end
19 October 2026
Key points
• September CPI to rise 0.62%mth 
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr 
from 4.0%yr. The seasonally adjusted 
CPI lifts 0.5%mth, a step down from 
the 0.7% increase in August.  
• September Trimmed Mean (TM) is 
set to lift 0.26%mth holding the 
annual pace steady at 3.6%yr. 
• As we noted in our review of the 
August CPI, our September quarter 
CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr 
while the TM remained 0.9%qtr.  
• Auto fuel surged ahead of our 
expectations so along with signs of 
increase pass-through we lifted our 
December quarter CPI to 1.3%qtr/3.9%yr 
and the TM estimate to 0.9%qtr/3.5%yr. 
• This is a 0.8ppt increase to the CPI 
estimate, and a 0.2ppt increase to the 
TM estimate, highlighting inflation 
momentum meaningfully above 
the RBA’s current expectations.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

was 1.9%, contributing 0.55ppt to the 0.8ppt increase in the 
quarterly estimate. Outside of a 0.09ppt contribution to the 
revision from holiday travel & accommodation (revised up from 
4.2% to 5.6%) all else was via small contributions for a large 
number of expenditure categories. This is why there is a 0.2ppt 
increase in our September quarter TM estimate from 0.7%qtr 
to 0.9%qtr.
A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-

expected fall in established home prices, could be the prover -
bial “straw” resulting in a larger than expected fall in demand 
and thus a more significant moderation in price setting behav -
iour. It is also possible that our auto fuel prices fall by more 
than we anticipate. For while we have spot crude prices to fall 
to about pre-crisis levels by end 2027, a combination of a slow 
recovery in refining capacity, constrained return of physical 
supplies and higher shipping cost keep Australian fuel prices 
above their pre-conflict level. Should all these factors improve 
fast than we expect, and auto fuel prices return to pre-conflict 
level if not lower, then headline inflation could dip below 2%yr 
and the TM would be closer to 2.5%yr (the mid point if the 
RBA’s inflation target), by end 2027.

《中東衝突升溫，地緣風險升國防優於航空運輸》
受到中東葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地多座機場 ，及烏
克蘭無人機攻擊俄羅斯能源設施導致布蘭特原油價
格10月再度攀升、帶動航空燃油價格同步上漲 ，高
油價再次衝擊航空運輸公司獲利。達美航空3Q26財
報，預告4Q26單位可用座位里程收入仍將增加，但
受到美國4Q26航空客運需求將下滑，以及航空燃油
價格上漲。達美航空也下調2026年全年獲利目標，
顯示地緣風險升溫再度衝擊航空運輸產業。
至於航空與國防產業受益於中東衝突與俄烏戰爭持
續消耗防空攔截器 、戰術飛彈及相關彈藥 ，帶動美

國及盟國由短期補庫存逐步轉向多年期採購 。全球
國防支出增加 、飛彈與防空系統補庫存 ，以及商用
航空引擎與維修需求持續成長 ，也讓產業巨頭雷神
(簡稱RTX)、奇異航天 (簡稱GE)、洛克希德馬丁 (簡
稱 LMT) 、 諾 斯 洛 普( 簡稱 NOC) 等 公 司 積 壓 訂 單
(Backlog)於3Q26均創歷史新高。

重點摘要
• 國防需求已由短期補庫存轉
為多年期擴產
《國防需求已由短期補庫存轉為多年期擴產》
美國主要航空與國防類股公布3Q26財報，中東衝突
再升溫導致國防需求高於商業航空 。以下是主要廠
商的分析 :
雷神∶受益於飛彈回補庫存 ，3Q26取得超過810億
美元商用與國防新訂單 ，其中國防訂單 590億美元 ，
包括約 230億美元 「戰斧 」飛彈訂單與 140億美元
「先進中程空對空飛彈 」（AMRAAM）訂單 ，帶
動總積壓訂單較上季增加 520億美元 ，累計訂單
3,400億美元 ，年增36%。在飛彈防空系統與商業
航空維修服務市場強勁帶動下 ，公司上調2026年營
收到973億美元、EPS 7.70美元(年增22%)。

洛克希德馬丁 ∶受益於飛彈與火控系統產量提升 ，
帶動3Q26營收與獲利優於預期。在PAC-3多年期合
約則推升下，積壓訂單增加616億美元達2,920億美
元。由於美國 、中東、北約國家飛彈補庫需求將持
續數年，所以公司上調2026年營收到810億美元(年
增8%)、EPS 30.55美元(年增42%)。
奇異航空 ∶受益於 LEAP交付 、備件 、維修及 shop
visit增加帶動 ，商用發動機及服務營收年增 21%，
3Q26營收優於預期。在商用航空備件、維修服務及
引擎交付需求優於預期 ，加上主要供應商材料投入
改善 ， 公司上調 2026 年營收到 508億美元 、EPS
7.75美元(年增26%)。
總結，雖然國防類股近期財報受益於產能提升維幅
上調全年財測 ，反觀油價高漲對於美國航空運輸業
造成全面性的衝擊，所以工業維持中立評等。
資料來源：公司，2026/10/28，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28

中國兩年期殖利率 1.25% 景氣下行壓力持續，本行仍預期接下來有降準50bp空間。

中國A 中立 中國景氣K型化加劇，投資方向維持選股不選市，聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈兼國產替代政策扶植的科技族群、稀缺原物料及高附加出口價值等產業。
香港 中立 削價競爭雖告尾聲但內需乏力影響獲利，並關注資本支出轉為獲利進程。
台灣 正向 長線台股AI供應鏈握技術進程優勢與漲價潮，擇評價面分批布局台股。

接下來的景氣下行壓力 。
《投資關鍵-中國經濟K型化情勢依舊》
縱觀8月中國經濟數據仍顯示外貿強，內需疲軟的景
象。中國8月出口增速從前月23.9%回升至25%，究
其細項，科技相關以名目計算年增速續保高速 ，如
自動資料處理機器零件年增76.5%， 儲存組件年增
159.2%，印刷電路板年增 44.4%與積體電路年增
129.8%。惟若以數量計算年增大多回落，顯示科技

8月出口增速佳來自價格貢獻。另一留意低端消費出
口也見改善，可觀察歐美消費旺季的挹注力道。
8月零售銷售持續疲軟年增回落至 0.4%，汽車零售
年衰退幅度擴大至-18.5%是主要拖累，零組件價格
上揚帶動通訊設備零售增27.3%。
8月實質工業生產年增略好預期至 5.2%，科技相關
保持較高能見度 ，通訊、電腦及其他電子設備年增
17.2%，但如汽車 、粗鋼與水泥較反應內需表現黯
淡，反內卷政策制約太陽能板等生產表現 。固定資
產投資年衰退幅度擴大至 -7.2%，房地產投資雙位
數衰退趨勢不變 ，製造業投資年減 -2.3%，我們認
為除反應信心不足 、內需疲軟外 ，反內卷的制約以
及政策傾斜高端科技是主因。
總體來看，中國經濟現階段來自價格為主的出口貢
獻，景氣越趨依賴全球科技週期 ，內需疲軟將視財
政執行力道進程才能緩解接下來的景氣下行壓力。
出口雖穩健，但內需與投資乏力，產能利用不振

《投資關鍵-台灣連十季利率不動 ，但升息壓力增 》
台灣央行第三季理監事會議宣布重貼現率、擔保放
款融通利率與短期融通利率維持不變在 2.0%、
2.375%和4.25%；可轉讓定期存單 (NCD) 28天期、
91 天期 、 182 天 期 也 維 持1.215% 、 1.275% 和
1.395% 不變。2024年以來，台灣央行持續保持利
率十連凍紀錄。
不動產信用管制部分 ，由於截至7月底，全體銀行
不動產貸款集中度續降至34.44%，低於2020年12

月底(第1度調整信用管制時點 )之 36.64%)，因此
調升自然人第 2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限至 7成。
同時也刪除購地貸款有關切結於一定期間內動工興
建之規定，相關規範回歸銀行依授信實務辦理。
資料來源：台灣央行，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09/18。

重點摘要
6
• 本行認為台灣景氣動能佳
且增長更加均衡 ，形成經
濟的正向循環 ，台灣經濟
環境已有宏觀的升息條件 。
年以來相較各國重返寬鬆貨幣政策列車 ，台灣依
舊維持基準利率 2%，2024/7各類新台幣存款準
備率調升 1碼。另觀察台灣 7月M2年增速 7.42%
(今年以來6.6%)雖高於政策目標區間 2.5%~6.5%，
但低於17.8%的名目 GDP 增長率，意味貨幣需求
增長速度超過貨幣供應量 ，因此台灣貨幣政策環
境已偏緊縮，所以並不需要立即升息。
展望後市，隨AI應用擴散至推論 、代理及終端裝
置推升算力需求 ，AI投資建設的暢旺驅動相關硬
體升級與用量成長 ，台灣企業涵蓋重要 AI供應鏈
節點，強勁出口引領經濟成長。

觀察AI科技紅利也逐步外溢其他產業 ，非科技如
機械出口逐步加溫 。相關半導體投資擴產需求積
極，投資成長也助力台灣經濟 ，內需也因股市財
富效應帶動。台灣景氣動能佳且增長更加均衡 ，
形成經濟的正向循環。
資料來源：主計處，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/08/07。

景。相較先前，央行將通膨發展情勢列為首要抉

並非主要來自供給測壓力，主要是考量核心CPI 已

的貢獻逐季顯現 ，這表明持續的內需復甦正在產

長表示 ，普發現金經費已納入總預算 ，立法院預
算審查進度將定奪普發現金 1萬的時點，目前可能
的時間最快在2027農曆春節（2月4日至10日）前
後，抑或在元宵節（2月20日）前。而根據央行預
估1Q27整體物價增速仍高於 2%，若疊加現金補
貼的加持 ，不排除物價增幅將更高於原預期水準 。
其次央行聲明第二個觀察重點 : 主要經濟體貨幣政
策緊縮程度。美歐日央行9月已升息一碼，美聯準
會年底尚有升息一碼的可能性 ，韓國央行 7、8月
連續性升息後，11月與1Q27都有可能再度升息。
多國相繼貨幣政策緊縮態勢 ，或增加台灣央行貨
幣政策抉擇的壓力 。而若台灣基準利率與他國利
差擴大 ，也可能引發資金外流 ，也不利央行關心
的金融情勢發展 。因此綜合來看 ，本行預期 12月
央行理監事會議有可能決議升息半碼至2.125%。
《投資關鍵-聚焦穩健基本面有望抵禦高利環境》
展望後市，美財政部有意擴大美債回購規模緩和債

務壓力，美聯準會9月升息一碼至3.75%-4.0%，年
底不排除還有一碼的升息空間，但從通膨與景氣預
估路徑來看，美聯準會的升息舉措並非典型的升息
循環，而是「不承諾路徑的風險管理式升息」。
回到對新興亞洲貨幣政策路徑來看，或許促使部分
新興亞洲央行政策光譜往緊縮端推移，但不改變原
具降息需求新亞國家的寬鬆方向，整體新亞國家貨
幣政策走向分歧。更重要的是決定新興亞洲貨幣政
策的考量點，如台灣與韓國主要是因應景氣回溫而
貨幣政策緊縮，但像無財政補貼物價致緊縮貨幣政
策國家如菲律賓，再者還有經濟成動能尚不穩固或
通膨可控，貨幣政策按兵不動的如中國、泰國等。

《地緣風險衝擊中東信用基本面 ，但緩衝能力仍佳 》
中東戰事持續外擴 ，除了美伊相互襲擊使得荷姆茲
海峽情勢依然緊張外 ，葉門胡塞武裝也對沙烏地阿
拉伯密集發動攻勢 ，柴油出口因此受阻 ，能源價格
處高位不墜 ，布蘭特原油 10月以來便維持在 100美
元/桶之上。過往中東國家往往是高油價的受惠者，
但受到能源生產和運輸中斷衝擊 ，國際貨幣基金10
月下調2026年中東及中亞地區經濟成長率預估值 1
個百分點至衰退，沙烏地阿拉伯更預估將衰退 3.3%。

人民幣債指數 2.20% 景氣不均衡復甦，寬鬆貨幣政策為主要支撐力

重點摘要 沙烏地阿拉伯 (以下簡稱沙國 )雖極力避免直接捲入
戰爭，但仍遭受衝突外溢效應波及 。戰爭不僅導致
沙國大幅增加補貼 ，軍事和安全支出以及融資費用
上升均加大財政支出 ，因此，即便油價上漲抵消產
量下降的影響而使石油收入高於預期，沙國2Q26預
算赤字佔 GDP比重仍達 7.1%，遠高於年度目標的
3.3%。儘管近期公布的 2027年預算計畫 (PBS)目標
是將預算赤字佔 GDP比率從2026年的4.9%(沙國官
方最新預估 )縮小到2027年的3.6%，但若戰爭長時
間 延 續， 此 目 標 或 難 以 達 成， 目 前 估 計2027 年
17420億里亞爾的債務目標亦將輕易被超越 。不過
即使如此 ，沙國目前負債占 GDP比重僅約 35%，

2027年市場最悲觀預期也大約40%左右，仍是低於
全球新興國家均值約80%的水準。
中東另一主要發債國卡達對天然氣出口依賴度高 ，
天然氣出口受阻也使得國際機構普遍預估卡達 2026
年經濟衰退10%以上、幾乎是2010年以來最重，所
幸卡達2026年以前累積巨額經常帳盈餘及外匯儲備
作為緩衝，即使在今年3月外部環境面臨極大壓力下，
卡達仍有 199億里亞爾的經常帳盈餘 ，累積過去四
季經常帳占 GDP比重仍達 13.5%，現今外匯存底足
以彌補3月的貿易逆差，並可維持４年以上。這些數
據都顯示戰爭對中東國家的經濟及對外償債能力確
實已帶來短線壓力 ，但過去累積的龐大緩衝使其具
備良好因應能力 ，信用違約交換 (CDS)目前並未因
定價油氣供應長期中斷而大幅攀升 ，今年雖持續發
債但債務壓力並不高 ，IMF更預期2027年中東國家
經濟將成長6.3%。然中東國家對能源出口依賴度仍
高，能源出口是否長期受阻則是後續觀察重點。

• 沙烏地阿拉伯負債占 GDP比
重約 35%低於全球新興國家

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《經濟強勁帶來的高利率無損新興債利差穩步收斂 》
財政收支並非驅動新興主權信用利差的唯一因素 ，
總體經濟環境 、市場流動性均牽動利差走勢 ，因此
雖然自7月以來沙烏地阿拉伯等中東國家信用利差又
再擴大，但也大概在3年均值附近並不算高。以歷史
經驗來看，BB級主權國家利差通常對財政惡化程度
反應較大 ，可能是因為該評等僅低於投資等級一級 ，
財政變化容易牽動投資人對升等至投資級的預期與
投資部位的調整 ，而在投資等級的主權國家中 ，則
以匈牙利和波蘭這些債務負擔較重的主權債利差較
易受財政問題影響。
油價和利率是近期2項重要觀察變數，也牽動利差走
勢，不過由於新興主權債指數中約5成的組成為大宗
商品出口國、3成為石油出口國，反而能受惠油價上

漲，油價上漲時與利差走勢敏感度也較油價下跌時
低；然而 ，更重要的是高油價對通膨和政策應對的
二階影響 ，將導致高利率長時間延續的可能性並最
終衝擊實體經濟 ，進一步限制石油出口國利差收窄 。
以長期趨勢來看 ，利率水準的絕對值高低不必然影
響新興債利差 ，利率走勢反而和信用利差呈現小幅
負相關，利率走升反映的若是強勁經濟成長而非能
源供給衝擊 ，此時往往能伴隨信用利差收窄 ，反而
需留意的是利率波動放大及利率的大幅重新定價 ，
這才是牽動利差波動加大的主因 。目前總體經濟環
境部分或可與2004~2007年相比擬，當時在強勁成
長的背景下利率長時間在4%~5.3%高位震盪，但新
興主要貨幣主權債利差仍可收斂至 134點(此波低點
189點)，若經濟成長能在現今高油價和高利率環境
下持續強勁，利差則可望保持穩定。
重點摘要
• BB級主權債及債務負擔沈重
的投資級主權債利差較容易
受財政問題干擾
• 新興主權債指數中約 5成為石
油出口國 ，降低油價上漲對
利差走勢的敏感性
3
即使油價上升，新興債利差與油價也少見正相關

重點摘要 《景氣及企業獲利續成長 ，企業債信改善趨勢延續 》
三大信評機構持續認可新興市場四大區域企業信用
評等的改善，3Q26新興市場企業債淨升等規模大幅
提高至760億美元，其中獲上調規模達到910億美元、
創下2025年第四季以來的最高水平 ，也已是連續第
5個季度改善。
3Q26新興市場4大區域評等調升數量均高於下調 ，
其中以亞洲改善動能最為顯著 ，主要貢獻來自於亞
洲金融業 ，全年升評 /降評比率已來到 1.8倍，逆轉
2019年降評多於升評的劣勢；拉美企業仍是新興市
場中違約率最高的區域 ，降評債券數量與其他區域
比也確實較高 ，但降評企業家數已是過去 10年來最
低，加上阿根廷主權債信獲上調帶動企業債 (主要是
國有企業)信評調升，拉美航空業者如南美航空集團

(LATAM Airlines)在油價維持高位環境下仍於3Q26
獲信評機構調升至 BB+，在企業債信分化環境下仍
有亮點 ，因此第三季拉美企業淨評級上調規模達
370億美元，反而創下2025年第三季以來的最佳季
度表現 ，全年共有 234家拉美企業信評得調升 。新
歐中東地區債信今年算是少數債信惡化的區域 ，很
大部分是受到戰爭及高油價影響所致 ，不過奈及利
亞和南非等主權評級上調仍帶來正向效果 ，梯瓦製
藥(Teva)評等更是上調至投資等級，帶來了120億美
元的貢獻，部分抵消地緣政治風險帶來的影響。
強勁的獲利成長持續支持新興企業穩健基本面 ，有
限的違約率輔以韌性十足的總經環境 、以及活躍但
不過度集中的初級市場 ，均是支持債信改善的原因 ，
預期在企業獲利仍看好的情境下 ，第四季企業債信
仍將朝正向發展。
• 3Q26新興市場企業債淨升等
規模大幅提高至760億美元，
創2025年第四季以來的最高
水平
• 新興市場4個主要區域評等調
升數量均高於下調 ，亞洲改
善動能最為顯著

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《聯準會升息年 ，新興債雖有波動但正報酬機率高 》
觀察過往聯準會升息年度 ，利率溫和走升機率相對
較高，新興債市雖然多會受到升息壓力而出現一波
跌勢，但全年度仍有較大機率收紅 ，1996年以來僅
有2018、2022這兩個升息年度新興債市全年收黑 。
不過2022年新興債市重挫很大一部分原因來自於聯
準會在短短一年時間內將基準利率自0％升至4.25%，
這種猛爆性升息與目前所預設的風險管理式的溫和
升息環境有所不同；另外 ，2018年聯準會雖只升息
四碼，但土耳其及阿根廷因美元稀缺引發流動性風
險，這2國經常帳赤字占 GDP比重當時均高於 5%，
對外融資壓力上升的同時進而爆發金融危機 。然而
現階段新興國家經常帳已普遍改善 ，土耳其經常帳

赤字已降至 2%~3%，阿根廷更轉為順差 ，基本面
與2018年有很大的差異。若扣除這兩段期間，新興
債市在聯準會升息期間波動雖可能放大 ，但在貨幣
政策方向更趨明朗後 ，走勢便會止穩反彈 ，全年平
均報酬率仍有10%以上。
至於新興市場企業債部分 ，過去因升息所造成的跌
勢約是主權債的一半 ，主要是因為企業債發行天期
多較主權要來得短 ，加上該期間獲利動能強勁 ，因
此在利率上升時期表現往往相對主權債更具韌性 。
儘管利率走勢仍存不確定性，但最終若能穩定在5%
左右，預估新興企業債全年總報酬仍可維持在3%，
除非未來出現更大的外部衝擊 ，如利率持續大幅上
升引發對經濟成長受損的擔憂 ，或AI驅動的成長模
式被重新評價 ，否則對新興債市的看法仍將維持正
向，新興主要貨幣主權債在這樣的大環境下 ，短線
雖相對承壓，但後續反彈力道也可望大於企業債。
• 新興主要貨幣主權債在聯準
會升息年度正報酬機率近8成
• 新興企業債相對抗跌 ，公債
利率若穩定在5%左右，全年
總報酬仍可維持在3%
5
資料來源： BofA ICE Indices、Bloomberg，統計1996~2026/10/9，中國信託商業銀行，

生産
7月の鉱工業生産（関西）は前月比－3.9%と4ヵ月ぶりにマイナスとなったが、均してみると持ち直しの動き
がみられる。汎用・生産用・業務用機械が増加した一方、化学、電子部品・デバイスなどが減少した。先行き

企業景況感（日銀短観）
日銀短観9月調査の業況判断DI（「良い」－「悪い」）は、全産業で＋21ptと6月調査（＋17ｐｔ）から改善。製
造業、非製造業ともに改善した。業種別に見ると、製造業では化学、非鉄金属、はん用・生産用・業務用機
械などが、非製造業は建設、物品賃貸、対個人サービスなどが改善している。先行き（12月）については、
製造業、非製造業ともに悪化が見込まれている。

マインド・景況感
9月の消費者態度指数（季節調整値）は34.7と3ヵ月連続で上昇した。
9月の景気ウォッチャー調査（現状判断DI、季節調整値）は前月差1.9pt低下の44.1となり、横ばいを示す
50を21ヵ月連続で下回った。秋物需要や連休・インバウンドで人出が増加したものの、物価高による消費
マインドの低下から景況感が悪化した。

## Document 535: 以色列大选结局倒计时：内塔尼亚胡阵营领先但仍缺多数，特殊选票或改写组阁格局

Source PDF: all report pdf/以色列大选结局倒计时：内塔尼亚胡阵营领先但仍缺多数，特殊选票或改写组阁格局.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

约 99% 的普通选票已完成统计，根据已统计选票预计，内塔尼亚胡阵营获 53 席，以国防军
前总参谋⻓艾森科特领导的反对派阵营获 48 席，阿拉伯政党合计获 19 席，⽽组阁需获议会
过半席位、即⾄少 61 席。内塔尼亚胡所属集团指控阿拉伯政党选举舞弊；以选举官员称⽬
前⽆依据认定存在系统性违规；以国家安全总局就可能的国外⼲预展开调查。组阁谈判或
持续数周，不排除再次举⾏⼤选。
摘要：
以⾊列议会选举进⼊最后计票阶段。随着约 99% 的普通选票完成统计，总理内塔尼亚胡领导的
右翼阵营暂时领先，但仍未获得组建多数政府所需的 61 席；反对派阵营同样未能过半，阿拉伯
政党则有望获得创纪录的 19 席，成为左右组阁谈判的关键⼒量。随着 43 万⾄ 48 万张特殊选票开
始清点，最终席位分配仍存变数，以⾊列下⼀届政府的组成也尚⽆定论。
据以⾊列及海外媒体报道，在⽬前已统计的选票中，内塔尼亚胡所属的利库德集团（ Lukid ）及
其右翼、极右翼和极端正统派盟友预计获得 53 席，以⾊列国防军前总参谋⻓艾森科特（ Gadi

Eisenkot ）领导的反对派阵营预计获得 48 席，阿拉伯政党合计预计获得 19 席。双⽅均未达到议
会多数⻔槛。
特殊选票清点于当地时间 10 ⽉ 29 ⽇周四凌晨开始，主要涉及以军⼠兵、外交⼈员、住院患者等
⽆法前往指定投票站的选⺠。选举委员会此前预计，此类选票约有 43 万⾄ 48 万张，最终结果可
能因此发⽣变化。
普通选票近乎点清，内塔尼亚胡阵营暂时领先
以⾊列中央选举委员会公布的初步计票结果显⽰，在绝⼤多数普通选票完成统计后，内塔尼亚
胡阵营暂时领先于艾森科特领导的反对派阵营，但领先优势尚不⾜以确保继续执政。
⽬前的席位预测显⽰，利库德集团及其右翼盟友合计获得 53 席，反对派阵营获得 48 席。阿拉伯
政党则有望获得 19 席，创下历史新⾼。
这⼀结果与最初的出⼝⺠调有所不同。随着实际选票陆续统计，内塔尼亚胡阵营的表现有所改
善，从最初出⼝⺠调显⽰的可能失去优势，转为暂时领先反对派。
不过，领先并不等于获得执政权。以⾊列议会共有 120 席，组建多数政府通常需要⾄少 61 席。按

照当前预测，内塔尼亚胡阵营仍缺 8 席，反对派阵营则缺 13 席。
这意味着，⽆论最终计票结果如何变化，各⽅都需要争取其他政党的⽀持，才能组建获得议会
多数⽀持的政府。
48 万张特殊选票待清点，最终席位仍存变数
本次选举的下⼀项关键进展，是特殊选票的清点。
以⾊列中央选举委员会此前预计，将有 43 万⾄ 48 万张 “ 双层信封 ” 特殊选票进⼊统计程序，低于此
前最⾼ 65 万张的准备规模。这类选票主要来⾃以军⼠兵、驻外外交⼈员、住院患者及其他⽆法
在指定投票站投票的选⺠。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

由于需要额外核实选⺠⾝份，并确认其未在其他地点重复投票，特殊选票通常晚于普通选票完
成统计。选举委员会于周四凌晨启动相关清点⼯作，因此截⾄⽬前，最终议席分配仍未完全确
定。
按照以⾊列的⽐例代表制，政党最终获得的席位取决于有效选票的分布，以及各党能否跨过
3.25% 的选举⻔槛。特殊选票不仅可能改变部分政党的得票⽐例，也可能影响接近选举⻔槛的⼩
党能否进⼊议会，从⽽间接改变各阵营的席位总数。
因此，尽管普通选票的统计已经接近完成，仍不能将⽬前的 53 席、 48 席和 19 席视为最终确认结
果。
阿拉伯政党或成组阁关键，反对派⾯临合作难题
阿拉伯政党有望获得 19 席，成为此次选举中影响组阁格局的重要⼒量。
从席位算术来看，如果艾森科特领导的反对派能够争取阿拉伯政党的⽀持，双⽅⽬前预计的席
位合计可达 67 席，超过 61 席的多数⻔槛。
但政治上的可⾏性远⽐简单相加复杂。
反对派内部在安全政策、宗教与世俗关系等问题上存在分歧，部分政党此前也承诺不会与阿拉
伯政党合作。阿拉伯政党与其他以⾊列政党在加沙战争、巴勒斯坦问题及国家安全政策上的⽴

场差异，同样可能阻碍联盟形成。
联合名单领导⼈优素福 · 贾巴林及其他阿拉伯政治⼈物已释放愿意协助结束内塔尼亚胡执政的信
号。不过，阿拉伯政党是否直接加⼊政府，还是仅在议会信任投票等关键环节提供⽀持，仍有
待谈判。
另⼀种可能是，由艾森科特领导的反对派组建少数政府，在不让阿拉伯政党直接进⼊内阁的情
况下，争取其在议会中的⽀持。但这⼀⽅案同样⾯临政治阻⼒。
对于内塔尼亚胡⽽⾔，当前的领先使其仍然拥有组阁主动权，但若坚持排除阿拉伯政党、同时
拒绝与部分反对派合作，其选择空间也将受到限制。
内塔尼亚胡指控选举舞弊，争议加剧政治对⽴
随着阿拉伯政党取得显著进展，围绕选举合法性的争议也在升温。
内塔尼亚胡所属的利库德集团指控阿拉伯政党可能存在选举舞弊，并提出有关贿选、恐吓选⺠
及外国势⼒⼲预的说法，但截⾄⽬前，相关指控尚未得到证实。
以⾊列选举官员表⽰，⽬前没有依据认定本次选举存在系统性违规。以⾊列国家安全总局已就
有关个别事件及可能的外国⼲预展开调查，但强调调查针对的是具体⾏为，⽽不是整个阿拉伯

选⺠群体。
阿拉伯政党领导⼈则批评，利库德集团试图通过质疑选举结果削弱阿拉伯选⺠的政治地位。反
对派也担⼼，这类指控可能增加与阿拉伯政党合作的政治成本，进⼀步压缩组阁空间。
选举争议由此与组阁谈判相互交织：阿拉伯政党获得的席位越多，其在议会中的潜在影响⼒越
⼤；但围绕其合法性的政治争论，也可能使其他政党更难公开接受其⽀持。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

组阁谈判或持续数周，不排除再次举⾏⼤选
即使特殊选票清点完成，以⾊列政坛也未必能够迅速摆脱僵局。
按照以⾊列的组阁程序，正式选举结果确认后，总统艾萨克 · 赫尔佐格将与各政党代表磋商，并
指定其认为最有可能组建新政府的候选⼈。获指定者需要在规定期限内争取⾜够的议会⽀持，
整个过程可能持续数周。
内塔尼亚胡⽬前仍担任看守总理。若最终计票结果没有明显改变各阵营的席位格局，他仍可能
凭借右翼阵营的领先地位争取组阁，但需要说服其他政党加⼊或⽀持其政府。
艾森科特则需要在反对派内部协调⽴场，并决定是否寻求阿拉伯政党的⽀持。如果双⽅都⽆法
取得⾜够的议会⽀持，以⾊列可能再次⾯临提前举⾏选举的局⾯。
以⾊列总统赫尔佐格已呼吁各党尽⼀切可能避免再次⼤选，但最终能否形成稳定政府，仍取决

于各⽅是否愿意在关键政策和政治安排上作出妥协。
⽬前的选举结果更接近⼀场没有明确赢家的议会⻆⼒：内塔尼亚胡阵营暂时领先，却仍缺少多
数席位；反对派拥有联合阿拉伯政党的潜在空间，却⾯临内部承诺与政治分歧的约束。随着特
殊选票进⼊清点阶段，以⾊列⼤选的最终席位格局即将明朗，但真正决定谁能执政的，仍将是
接下来的组阁谈判。
